4.
Marked semisimplicial sets

The development of the homotopy theory of marked semisimplicial sets will be largely analogous to the unmarked case.
As such, we will be less verbose and stress the differences to the unmarked case.

4.1.
Basic notions

4.1.1.
Marked semicategories

We have a category
\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}}
of
marked semicategories
, i.e. semiscategories equipped with a subset of edges called marked.
We have a forgetful functor
\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}}\to\mathbf{SemiCat}
part of an adjoint quadruple that will be denoted as for marked semisimplicial sets.
The symmetric monoidal structure of
\mathbf{SemiCat}
extends to
\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}}
.
All functors in the adjoint quadruple between
\mathbf{SemiCat}
and
\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}}
lift canonically to symmetric monoidal functors.

We have a canonical functor
\mathbf{Cat}\to\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}}
that sends a category to the underlying semicategory with those morphisms marked that were isomorphisms in the original category.
This functor is fully faithful and its image contains exactly those marked semicategories
(\mathcal{C},W)
such that pre- and postcomposition with marked morphisms is a bijection and every object appears as the domain of a marked morphism.
It has a symmetric monoidal left adjoint
(\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}},\top,\otimes)\to(\mathbf{Cat},1,\times)
sending a semicategory to the free category localized at the marked morphisms.

4.1.2.
Marked semisimplicial sets

A
marking

W
of a semisimplicial set
A
is given by a subset of its edges.
A morphism
(A,W_{A})\to(B,W_{B})
of marked semisimplicial sets is a morphism
f\colon A\to B
of semisimplicial maps such that
f(W_{A})\subseteq W_{B}
.
We obtain a category
(\widehat{\Delta_{+}})_{\mathsf{m}}
of marked semisimplicial sets.
It can equivalently be described as the full category of separated presheaves on the category
\Delta_{+}^{\prime}
that under
\Delta_{+}
has an object
[1]_{\mathsf{m}}
and a morphism
[1]\to[1]_{\mathsf{m}}
freely added and the only non-trivial cover given by the singleton cover at that morphism.
Like
\Delta_{+}
, note that
\Delta_{+}^{\prime}
is a direct category.
We have a retraction
\Delta_{+}^{\prime}\to\Delta_{+}
that sends the morphism
[1]\to[1]_{\mathsf{m}}
to the identity on
[1]
.

Remark 4.1
.

We could just as well work with presheaves over
\Delta_{+}^{\prime}
as our notion of marked semisimplicial set, with the added benefits of a presheaf category.
However, this is still not enough to define classifiers for fibrations.
For this, we would want that codomains of generating anodyne maps are representable.
This can be achieved by working with presheaves over
\Delta_{\mathsf{m}}
, the wide subcategory of marked non-empty finite total linear orders restricted to monomorphisms.
The theory works just as well there, although with generating anodyne maps suitably enlarged: marked semisimplicial sets are a full subcategory of sheaves on
\Delta_{\mathsf{m}}
; one now turns the sheaf condition into weak sheaf condition for fibrant objects.
In fact, one can do the same for weak semicomplicial sets: here, the nicest setting would be presheaves over the full subcategory of semisimplicial sets on cofibrations with representable codomain.
∎

We write
\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathsf{m}}
for the category of marked simplicial sets, defined similarly to marked semisimplicial sets but with degenerate edges always marked.
Note that it can be equivalently described as the full subcategory of separated presheaves on the category
\Delta^{\prime}
that under
\Delta
has a factorization
[1]\to[1]_{\mathsf{m}}\to[0]
of
s_{0}
freely added and the only non-trivial cover given by the singleton cover
[1]\to[1]_{\mathsf{m}}
.
Like
\Delta
, note that
\Delta^{\prime}
is an elegant Reedy category; its direct fragment is
\Delta_{+}^{\prime}
nad its inverse fragment consists of
\Delta_{-}
together with the map
[1]_{\mathsf{m}}\to[0]
.
We have a retraction
\Delta^{\prime}\to\Delta
that sends the morphism
[1]\to[1]_{\mathsf{m}}
to the identity on
[1]
.
This section-retraction pair is compatible with the Reedy structures.

We have a forgetful functor from marked simplicial sets to marked semisimplicial sets given by precomposition with the evident inclusion
\Delta_{+}^{\prime}\to\Delta^{\prime}
.
This has a left adjoint, the
free marked simplicial set
functor, and a right adjoint, the
cofree marked simplicial set
functor.

We denote
(-)^{\mathsf{u}}
the forgetful functor from marked semisimplicial sets to semisimplicial sets.
It has as adjoints on the left the
minimal marking

(-)^{\flat}
and on the right the
maximal marking

(-)^{\sharp}
.
We will usually leave the minimal marking functor implict, regarding a semisimplicial set as minimally marked by default.
The maximal marking functor has a further right adjoint
\mathsf{core}
that returns the restriction of the underlying semisimplicial set to those simplices all of whose edges are marked.
We have a corresponding quadruple of adjoint functors in the simplicial case and will use the same notation for it.

Note that forgetting the marking commutes (strictly) with forgetting degeneracy operators.
This commuting square of functors satisfies a number of Beck-Chevalley conditions:

•

The free (marked) simplicial set functor commutes with minimal marking, forgetting the marking, maximal marking, and taking the core.

•

The forgetful functor from (marked) simplicial to (marked) semisimplicial sets does not commute with minimal marking, but does commute with maximal marking, fogetting the marking, and taking the core.

•

The cofree (marked) simplicial set functor does not commute with forgetting the marking, but does commute with maximal marking and taking the core.

We extend the closed geometric symmetric monoidal structure from semisimplicial sets to marked semisimplicial sets by letting those edges in
(A,W_{A})\otimes(B,W_{B})
be marked whose edge components in
A
or
B
are marked.
We use the same notation for it as in semisimplicial sets.
All functors in the adjoint quadruple between semisimplicial sets and marked semisimplicial sets lift canonically to symmetric monoidal functors with respect to the geometric symmetric monoidal structures.
As in the unmarked case, the free marked simplicial set functor is symmetric monoidal with respect to the geometric symmetric monoidal structure on marked semisimplicial sets and the cartesian monoidal structure on marked simplicial sets.

Note that the fully faithful functor
\Delta_{+}\to\mathbf{SemiCat}
extends to a fully faithful functor
\Delta_{+}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}}
that sends
[1]_{+}
to the semicategory
[1]
with maximal marking.
We have a strictly commuting diagram

\textstyle{\Delta_{+}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{SemiCat}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\Delta_{+})^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}}}

where the vertical arrows are the evident inclusion and the minimal marking functor.
The functor
\Delta_{+}^{\prime}\to\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}}
gives rise to a nerve
\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}}\to\widehat{\Delta_{+}^{\prime}}
valued in separated presheaves, thus lifting to a fully faithful functor
\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}}\to(\widehat{\Delta_{+}})_{\mathsf{m}}
we call the
nerve

N
for marked semisimplicial sets.
Both the nerve and its realization left adjoint commute up to canonical natural isomorphism with the adjoint quadruples for marked semicategories and marked semisimplicial sets.

The geometric symmetric monoidal structure of
\mathbf{SemiCat}
extends to
\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}}
by letting those morphisms in
(\mathcal{C},W_{\mathcal{C}})\otimes(\mathcal{D},W_{\mathcal{D}})
be marked whose morphisms components in
\mathcal{C}
or
\mathcal{D}
are marked.
All functors in the adjoint quadruple between
\mathbf{SemiCat}
and
\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}}
lift canonically to symmetric monoidal functors with respect to the geometric symmetric monoidal structures.
The marked semisimplicial nerve and its realization are symmetric monoidal functors with respect to the geometric symmetric monoidal structures.

As in the unmarked case, we can understand the geometric symmetric monoidal structure as being induced via argumentwise cocontinuous extension (a generalized Day convolution process) by the geometric symmetric monoidal structure on marked semicategories.

4.1.3.
Join and slice

We extend the join monoidal structure from semisimplicial sets to marked simplicial sets, also reusing notation, by marking only those edges in a join that were already marked in its arguments.
The functor
(-)^{\flat},(-)^{\mathsf{u}},\mathsf{core}
lift to monoidal functors between join monoidal structures.
Occasionally, we will have use also for the
marking join monoidal structure

(0,\star_{\mathsf{m}})
, which additionally marks the newly introduced edges going between vertices of different arguments.
The functors
(-)^{\mathsf{u}},(-)^{\sharp},\mathsf{core}
lift to monoidal functors between the join monoidal structure on simplicial sets and the marking join monoidal structure.
Given a marked simplicial set
A
, the functors

-\star A,A\star-,-\star_{\mathsf{m}}A,A\star_{\mathsf{m}}-\colon\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathsf{m}}\to(\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathsf{m}})_{\backslash A}

admit right adjoints.
Their respective action on an object
p\colon A\to X
is written

X_{/p},X_{\backslash p},X_{{\mathsf{m}}/p},X_{{\mathsf{m}}\backslash p}.

For a cofibration
i\colon A\to B
, we have a functor

\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathsf{m}}\to(\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathsf{m}})_{\backslash B}

sending
X
to the pushout

\textstyle{A\star X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i\star X}
\textstyle{A\star_{\mathsf{m}}X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{B\star X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\bullet\ignorespaces\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

This functor has a right adjoint.
Its action on an object
p\colon B\to X
is written
X_{/(p,pi)}
.
It is equivalently obtained as the pullback

\textstyle{X_{/(p,pi)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{/p}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{{\mathsf{m}}/pi}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X_{/pi}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

Dually, we define
X_{\backslash(p,pi)}
.

Everything we have said in this paragraph extends to marked semisimplicial sets as well.
We use the same notation in that setting.
The forgetful functor marked simplicial to marked semisimplicial sets is monoidal with respect to join as well as marking join.
The induced oplax monoidal structures of the free marked simplicial set functor are monoidal structures.

4.2.
Weak factorization systems

The
cofibrations
of marked semisimplicial sets are those maps that are cofibrations (i.e. levelwise decidable monomorphisms) on underlying semisimplicial sets and additionally consist of a decidable inclusion of markings.
They are generated under weak saturation by boundary inclusions and the
edge marking inclusion

(\Delta^{1})^{\flat}\to(\Delta^{1})^{\sharp}
, concretely write as
\omega
-compositions of pushouts of coproducts of boundary inclusions and the edge marking inclusion.
Note that every object is cofibrant.

Lemma 4.2
.

Cofibrations are closed under the Leibniz geometric symmetric monoidal structure.

Proof.

Analogous to
lemma

3.4
.
∎

Lemma 4.3
.

Cofibrations are closed under the Leibniz join monoidal structure.

Proof.

Analogous to
lemma

3.5
.
The Leibniz join of an edge marking inclusion with a boundary inclusion is a relative cell complex of edge marking inclusions.
∎

Lemma 4.4
.

Cofibrations are closed under the Leibniz marking join monoidal structure.

Proof.

Analogous to
lemma

4.3
.
∎

The forgetful functor to semisimplicial sets and the minimal and maximal marking functors preserve cofibrations.

Let us recall some terminology in semisimplicial sets.
For
n\geq 1
and
0\leq k\leq n
, the horn
h_{k}^{n}\colon\Lambda_{k}^{n}\to\Delta^{n}
is called
inner
if
0<k<n
, else outer (left outer if
k=0
and right outer if
k=n
).
The
critical edge
of an outer horn
h_{k}^{n}\colon\Lambda_{k}^{n}\to\Delta^{n}
is given by the distance preserving map
[1]\to[n]
preserving the initial object if
k=0
and the terminal object if
k=n
; we regard it as part of
\Lambda^{n}_{k}
for
n\geq 2
.
Inner horns do not have a critical edge.

The
marked horn inclusions
are given by minimally marked inner horn inclusions together with outer horn inclusions with only the critical edge marked.
The
marking saturation inclusion
is given by
(\Delta^{3},\{02,13\})\to(\Delta^{3})^{\sharp}
.
It will correspond to a saturation condition, specifically the 2-out-of-6 property.
The marked horn inclusions and marking saturation inclusion generate the weak factorization system (anodyne, fibration).
The forgetful functor to semisimplicial sets and the maximal marking functor presserve anodyne maps.

Remark 4.5
.

Following up on
remark

3.6
, the choice of these generators is even more arbitrary than in the unmarked case.
For example, we could have added generators such as
(\Delta^{2},\{01,02\})\to(\Delta^{2})^{\sharp}
corresponding to a 2-out-of-3 property.
As per
lemmas

4.9
and
4.10
(and
corollary

4.22
for other generators), this would not change the fibrant objects and fibrations between fibrant objects.
Ultimately, for the purpose of developing the homotopy theory of marked semisimplicial sets, we will only care about fibrations that have fibrant codomain (recall that in the theory of quasicategories, fibrations are also only characterized explicitly when they have a quasicategory as codomain).
Thus, we have chosen the least amount of generators needed to characterize these.
∎

Lemma 4.6
.

Anodyne maps are closed under Leibniz geometric product with cofibrations.

Proof.

This is analogous to
lemma

3.7
.

Let us make sure that the anydone map (
3.2
) from the unmarked case remains anodyne in the current marked setting.
If
a_{0},a_{1}\geq 0
, this is a (generalized) inner horn, which is anodyne also in the marked case.
Recall that
a_{0}=a_{1}=-1
is impossible.
Let now treat the case
a_{0}=-1
and
a_{1}\geq 0
(the case
a_{0}\geq 0
and
a_{1}=-1
is dual).
Since
u_{0}
is surjective, we must have
m_{0}=-1
, i.e.
k=0
.
Thus, we are in the case of the pushout product of the horn inclusion
\Lambda_{0}^{m}\to\Delta^{m}
with edge from
0
to
1
marked with the boundary inclusion
\partial\Delta^{n}\to\Delta^{n}
.
In particular, the relevant marking on
\Delta^{m}\otimes\Delta^{n}
contains the edge from
(0,i)
to
(1,i)
for
i\in[n]
.

Observe that
E
is the singleton subposet of
[a]
just consisting of the greatest element
a
.
If
a\geq 1
, then (
3.2
) is an inner horn, so asume
a=0
.
Now (
3.2
) is the outer horn
\Lambda^{a_{1}+1}_{0}\to\Delta^{a_{1}+1}
, so we have to make sure that the edge from
0
to
1
is mapped to an edge of
\Delta^{m}\otimes\Delta^{n}
that is marked.
Indeed, it is sent to the edge from
(0,v_{1}(0))
to
(1,v_{1}(0))
.
∎

Lemma 4.7
.

Anodyne maps are closed under Leibniz join with cofibrations.
Anodyne maps are closed under join with marked semisimplicial sets.

Proof.

Analogous to
lemma

3.9
.
∎

Lemma 4.8
.

Anodyne maps are closed under Leibniz marking join with cofibrations.

Proof.

Analogous to
lemma

3.9
.
∎

We have corresponding adjoint closure statements for fibrations.

A map between fibrant objects that lifts against cofibrations is called a
trivial fibration
.

4.3.
Fundamental category

The explicit construction of the fundamental groupoid of a fibrant semisimplicial sets extends more or less mechanically to an explicit construction of the fundamental category of a fibrant marked semisimplicial set.
We note only the differences.
For this subsection, fix a fibrant marked semisimplicial set
X
.

A
constant loop
in
X
is a constant loop in
\mathsf{core}(X)
.
Recall that
\mathsf{core}(X)
is a fibrant semisimplicial set, so the discussion of constant loops from the unmarked case applies.

Recall the maximal marking monad
((-)^{\mathsf{u}})^{\sharp}
.
The following lemma shows that markings in fibrant marked semisimplicial sets also have a 2-out-of-3 property.

Lemma 4.9
.

Fibrant objects lift against the Leibniz application of the unit of the maximal marking monad to 2-dimensional horn inclusions.
Concretely, given fibrant
X
and a triangle
\sigma\in X_{2}(f,g,h)
with
f\in X_{1}(a,b),g\in X_{1}(b,c),h\in X_{1}(a,c)
, if two out of
f,g,h
are marked, then so is the third.

Proof.

We will show that
h
is marked if
f
and
g
are.
The other cases are analogous.

Recall the anodyne map
j\colon\Delta^{0}\to L\to\Delta^{0}\star L
in semisimplicial sets.
Using
lemma

4.7
, we have that

\textstyle{\Delta^{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Delta^{0}\star j^{\sharp}\star\Delta^{0}}
\textstyle{\Delta^{0}\star(\Delta^{0}\star L)^{\sharp}\star\Delta^{0}}

is anodyne.
Extending
\sigma
along this map and then restricting along the map induced by
\Delta^{1}\to L\to\Delta^{0}\star L
, we obtain a tetrahedron
\Delta^{3}\to X
that has
f
as
02
-edge,
g
as
13
-edge, and
h
as
03
-edge.
Lifting against the marking saturation inclusion, we deduce that
h
is marked.
∎

Remark 4.10
.

Fibrations between fibrant objects also have the lifting property of
lemma

4.9
.
This follows abstractly since the maps lifted against are epi.
∎

Let
{\mathsf{a}},{\mathsf{b}}
denote the vertices of
P
, in this order.
We say that parallel edges
f,g\in X_{1}(a,b)
forming a map
[f,g]\colon P\to X
are
related
, written
f\approx g
, if
P\to X
lifts to a map

(\Delta^{0}\star P,\{\Delta^{0}\star a\})\to X.

Equivalently, the marked semisimplicial set
X_{/([f,g],a)}
contains a vertex.
Note that
X_{/(f,a)}
and
X_{/(g,a)}
are trivially fibrant and that
X_{/([f,g],a)}\to X_{/(f,a)}
and
X_{/([f,g],a)}\to X_{/(g,a)}
are fibrations.
Thus, if
f\approx g
, then the latter two maps are surjective on vertices.

Note that we have anodyne maps as indicated:

\textstyle{P\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\Delta^{0}\star P,\{\Delta^{0}\star a\})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{anod}}
\textstyle{(P\star\Delta^{0},\{b\star\Delta^{0}\})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{anod}}
\textstyle{(\Delta^{0}\star P\star\Delta^{0},\{\Delta^{0}\star a,b\star\Delta^{0}\})\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

As before, this implies that the notion of relatedness is invariant under taking opposites.

Just as before, one sees that relatedness is an equivalence relation.
Using
lemma

4.9
, one sees that markings are invariant under relatedness.

Recall the anodyne map
P\to Q
from the unmarked case.
It was constructed as the functorial action of taking cokernels on the map of arrows from
\partial\Delta^{1}\to\Delta^{1}
to
\Delta^{0}+\Delta^{1}\to\Delta^{2}
.
This map is Reedy left anodyne, where left anodyne means the weak saturation of inner and left horn inclusions.
It follows that
P\to Q
is left anodyne, hence that
\Delta^{0}\star P\to\Delta^{0}\star Q
is inner anodyne.
Given triangles
\sigma_{0}\in X_{2}(f_{0},g,h_{0})
and
\sigma_{1}\in X_{2}(f_{1},g,h_{1})
with first vertex
a
, we form a map
[\sigma_{0},\sigma_{1}]\colon Q\to X
and then have a span

\textstyle{X_{/([\sigma_{0},\sigma_{1}],a)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{triv}}
\textstyle{X_{/([f_{0},f_{1}],a)}}
\textstyle{X_{/([h_{0},h_{1}],a)}\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

It follows that
f_{0}\approx f_{1}
implies
h_{0}\approx h_{1}
.
We obtain a dual statement for triangles that share the
d_{2}
-indexed edge.
Combining these using transitivity, given triangles
\sigma_{0}\in X_{2}(f_{0},g_{0},h_{0})
and
\sigma_{1}\in X_{2}(f_{1},g_{1},h_{1})
, if
f_{0}\approx f_{1}
and
g_{0}\approx g_{1}
, then
h_{0}\approx h_{1}
.

The
fundamental category

\tau_{1}X
is defined analogously to the groupoidal case.
The objects are given by vertices
X_{0}
and the morphisms from
a
to
b
are given by the quotient
X_{1}(a,b)/{\approx}
of edges under relatedness.
Composition is given by fillings of
h^{2}_{1}
-horns.
The identity on
a\in X_{0}
is given by a constant loop on
a
.
This makes
\tau_{1}X
into a category.

Note that marked edges represent isomorphisms by fillings of marked outer 2-dimensional horns.
Furthermore, any edge that represents an isomorphism is marked by lifting against the marking saturation inclusion and invariance of markings under relatedness.
Thus, an edge is marked precisely if it represents an isomorphism.

4.4.
Enrichment in categories

We have a nerve

\textstyle{\mathbf{Cat}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{N}
\textstyle{(\widehat{\Delta_{+}})_{\mathsf{m}}}

from categories.
Its left adjoint is the
fundamental category
functor
\tau_{1}
.
Given a marked semisimplicial set
A
, one may describe
\tau_{1}A
as the free category on the graph
A_{1}\rightrightarrows A_{0}
quotiented under the relation on morphisms given by triangles
A_{2}
and further localized at morphisms coming from marked edges.
It thus depends only on the truncation of
A
to three levels (plus markings).

If
A
is fibrant, we see that
\tau_{1}A
as defined here coincides up to isomorphism with
\tau_{1}A
as defined in the previous subsection.
Note for non-fibrant
A
, an isomorphism in
\tau_{1}A
does not need to come from a zig-zag of marked edges in
A
.

Since

\tau_{1}\colon((\widehat{\Delta_{+}})_{\mathsf{m}},\top,\otimes)\to(\mathbf{Cat},1,\times)

is symmetric monoidal, we can regard marked semisimplicial sets as enriched in categories with the hom-category from
A
to
B
given by
\tau_{1}(\hom(A,B))
(note that
\hom(A,B)
is fibrant if
B
is).
When restricted to to fibrant objects, we will also call this the
homotopy 2-category
of marked semisimplicial sets.
It is analogous to the 2-category of quasicategories of
[
RV15
]
, but has less strict structure (for example, it is not cartesian closed, but only has a weak notion of exponential).
In order to avoid excessive overloading of notation, identities, composition, and inverses in hom-categories will be written
\operatorname{id}_{2}
,
-\circ_{2}-
, and
\operatorname{inv}_{2}
.

In the category of categories, let us call cofibrations the functors whose action on objects is a decidable monomorphism, fibrations the isofibrations, and weak equivalences the equivalences.
Recall that this forms a model category.

We have that
\tau_{1}
maps cofibrations and anodyne maps of marked semisimplicial sets to cofibrations and trivial cofibrations of categories, respectively: by cocontinuity, it suffices to check this for the respective generators; for these, it is clear.

We also have that
\tau_{1}
maps fibrations between fibrant objects of marked semisimplicial sets to fibration of categories: given a fibration
Y\to X
of marked semisimplicial sets, an isomorphism
a\to b
in
\tau_{1}(X)
and a lift
y
of
b
to
\tau_{1}(Y)
, recall that
a\to b
is represented by a marked edge from
a
to
b
; using a marked
h^{1}_{1}
-horn lifting, we can it to
Y
.
In contrast to the groupoidal case, this observation fails for fibrations with non-fibrant codomain.

Given
X\in\widehat{\Delta_{+}}
and
f,g\in X_{1}(a,b)
, we call
f
and
g

related
, written
f\approx g
, if
f,g\colon\Delta^{1}\to X
become equal after applying
\tau_{1}
.
If
X
is fibrant, we see that this notion of relatedness coincides with the one defined in the previous subsection.

Lemma 4.11
.

Let
p\colon Y\to X
be a fibration in marked semisimplicial sets.
Given
f\in Y_{1}(a,b)
and
\overline{g}\in X_{1}(pa,pb)
such that
pf\approx\overline{g}
, there is
g\in Y_{1}(a,b)
such that
pg=\overline{g}
and
f\approx g
.

Proof.

After taking a fibrant replacement of
X
, we see that the claim reduces to the case where
X
is fibrant.
In that case, we way work with the explicit description of
\tau_{1}Y
and
\tau_{1}X
from the previous subsection.
The claim then follows by solving a lifting problem

\textstyle{\Delta^{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{anod}}
\textstyle{Y\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{(\Delta^{0}\star P,\Delta^{0}\star\{a\})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{X}

where the bottom map is the witness of
pf\approx pg
and the left map comes from one of the inclusions
\Delta^{1}\to P
and is observed to be anodyne.
∎

4.5.
Homotopies

The
interval
in marked semisimplicial sets is imported from semisimplicial sets via maximal marking.
As before, the interval together with the geometric monoidal structure induces a notion of
homotopy

h\colon f_{0}\sim f_{1}
between maps
f_{0},f_{1}\colon X\to Y
of marked semisimplicial sets that is in bijection with marked edges between the transposes
\overline{f_{0}}
and
\overline{f_{1}}
in
\hom(X,Y)
(i.e. an edges in
\mathsf{core}(\hom(X,Y))
between the imgea of
\overline{f_{0}}
and
\overline{f_{1}}
).

If
Y
is fibrant, such a marked edge exists precisely if there is an isomorphism between the images of
\overline{f_{0}}
and
\overline{f_{1}}
in
\tau_{1}(\hom(X,Y))
.
Thus, the existence of homotopies with fibrant target can be tested in the categorical enrichment of marked semisimplicial sets.

Following Riehl-Verity, when working with homotopies targetting fibrant objets, we will freely exploit the relation to the categorical enrichment and regard homotopies as idomorphisms in hom-categories with relatedness of homotopies given by equality in the hom-category, yielding notions of horizontal composition and vertical composition and inversion for homotopies that is closed under relatedness.
Given an isomorphism in
\hom(B,C)
represented by homotopy
H\colon I\otimes B\to C
, note that the right whiskering with an object of
\hom(A,B)
given by a map
f\colon A\to B
is represented by
H\circ(I\otimes f)
and that the left whiskering with an object of
\hom(C,D)
given by a map
g\colon C\to D
is represented by
g\circ H
.

As before, we have a notion of
homotopy equivalence
.
Homotopy equivalences become actual equivalences in the categorical enrichment of marked semisimplicial sets (and come from those if
A
and
B
are fibrant).
As before, we say this homotopy equivalence is
coherent
if
f\circ H\approx K\circ(I\otimes f)
.

Lemma 4.12

(Graduate Lemma)
.

For any homotopy equivalence
(f,g,H,K)
as above, there is a replacement
K^{\prime}
of
K
such that
(f,g,H,K^{\prime})
forms a coherent homotopy equivalence.

Proof.

Same proof as
lemma

3.14
.
∎

As before, we have the notion of
strong homotopy equivalence
.

4.6.
Homotopy theory

We obtain statements as in
section

3.6
.
The omitted proofs are adapted verbatim.

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
(f,g,H,K)
form a coherent homotopy equivalence between objects
U
and
V
with
V
fibrant.

(i)

If
f
is a fibration, there is
H^{\prime}\approx H
such that
(f,g,H^{\prime},K)
forms a strong homotopy equivalence,

(ii)

If
f
is a cofibration, there is
K^{\prime}\approx K
such that
(f,g,H,K^{\prime})
forms a strong homotopy equivalence.
∎

Corollary 4.14
.

The following are equivalent for a cofibration
m
between fibrant objects:

(i)

m
is a homotopy equivalence,

(ii)

m
is a strong homotopy equivalence,

(iii)

m
is anodyne.
∎

Corollary 4.15
.

The following are equivalent for a fibration
p
between fibrant objects:

(i)

p
is a homotopy equivalence,

(ii)

p
is a strong homotopy equivalence,

(iii)

p
is a trivial fibration.
∎

Since equivalences in a 2-category satisfy 2-out-of-6, so do homotopy equivalences between fibrant objects.
Thus,
corollaries

4.14
and
4.15
show that the full subcategory
(\widehat{\Delta_{+}})_{{\mathsf{m}},{\mathsf{fib}}}
of marked semisimplicial sets on fibrant objects form a model structure (cofibrations, homotopy equivalences, fibrations).

Theorem 4.16
.

Fibrant marked semisimplicial sets form a fibration category.

Proof.

Analogous to
theorem

3.18
.
∎

The notion of homotopy equivalence between fibrant objects extends to a notion of
weak equivalence
between arbitrary marked semisimplicial sets as in
section

3.6
.

Lemma 4.17
.

Consider a diagram

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{anod}}
\scriptstyle{j}
\textstyle{X\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f}
\scriptstyle{g}
\textstyle{Y}

with
X
and
Y
fibrant,
j
anodyne, and
jf=jg
.
Then
f
and
g
are homotopic.
∎

Corollary 4.18
.

Given a span

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{anod}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{anod}}
\textstyle{X_{1}}
\textstyle{X_{2}}

with
X_{1}
and
X_{2}
fibrant, there is a map
X_{1}\to X_{2}
under
A
that is a homotopy equivalence.
∎

Lemma 4.19
.

Given a commuting diagram

\textstyle{X_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{anod}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{anod}}
\textstyle{X_{2}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{Y_{1}}
\textstyle{B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{anod}}
\scriptstyle{\mathsf{anod}}
\textstyle{Y_{2}}

with anodyne maps and fibration as indicated such that
X_{1},X_{2},Y_{1},Y_{2}
are fibrant, if
X_{1}\to Y_{1}
is a homotopy equivalence, then so is
X_{2}\to Y_{2}
.
∎

Corollary 4.20
.

Marked semisimplicial sets form a homotopical category, i.e. weak equivalences satisfy 2-out-of-6.
∎

Lemma 4.21
.

Weak equivalences lift against fibrant objects up to homotopy.
∎

Corollary 4.22
.

Cofibrations that are weak equivalences lift against fibrations between fibrant objects.
∎

A marked semisimplicial set is called
finite
if its underlying semisimplicial set is finite.

Lemma 4.23
.

In the full subcategory of finite marked semisimplicial sets, the class of weak equivalences is the closure under 2-out-of-6 of pushouts of marked horn inclusions.
∎

Lemma 4.24
.

Homotopies are closed under limit and colimit, i.e. the limit and colimit functors from
[\mathcal{D},(\widehat{\Delta_{+}})_{\mathsf{m}}]
to
(\widehat{\Delta_{+}})_{\mathsf{m}}
preserve the homotopy relation.
∎

Corollary 4.25
.

Homotopies are closed under product and coproduct (of arbitrary arity), i.e. if
f_{i}\sim g_{i}
for all
i\in I
, then
\prod_{i\in I}f_{i}\sim\prod_{i\in I}g_{i}
and
\coprod_{i\in I}f_{i}\sim\coprod_{i\in I}g_{i}
.
∎

Corollary 4.26
.

Homotopy equivalences are closed under product and coproduct (of arbitrary arity).
∎

Lemma 4.27
.

The functors
-\otimes-
and
\hom(-,-)
preserve homotopies in each arguments.
∎

Corollary 4.28
.

The functors
-\otimes-
and
\hom(-,-)
preserve homotopy equivalences in each arguments.
∎

Corollary 4.29
.

For
X
fibrant, the functor
\hom(-,X)
preserves weak equivalences.
∎

Corollary 4.30
.

For
X
fibrant, the functor
\hom(-,X)
sends cofibrations that are weak equivalences to trivial fibrations.
∎

Lemma 4.31
.

The following are equivalent for a map
f
:

(i)

f
is a weak equivalence,

(ii)

\hom(f,X)
is a homotopy equivalence for all fibrant
X
,

(iii)

\tau_{0}(\hom(f,X))
is a bijection all fibrant
X
, i.e. precomposition with
f
induces a bijection of homotopy classses when the target is fibrant.

∎

Corollary 4.32
.

A cofibration
m
is a weak equivalence exactly if the fibration
\hom(m,Z)
is trivial for all fibrant
Z
.
∎

Corollary 4.33
.

Anodyne maps are weak equivalences.
∎

Corollary 4.34
.

Weak equivalences are the closure of anodyne maps, more specifically just
\omega
-compositions of pushouts of coproducts of generating anodyne maps, under 2-out-of-6.
∎

Corollary 4.35
.

Homotopy equivalences are weak equivalences.
∎

Corollary 4.36
.

Weak equivalences are closed under coproduct (of arbitrary arity).
∎

Corollary 4.37
.

Weak equivalences are closed under retract.
∎

Lemma 4.38
.

Weak equivalences that are cofibrations are closed under pushout as well as
\omega
-composition.
∎

Theorem 4.39
.

Marked semisimplicial sets form a cofibration category.

Proof.

Analogous to
theorem

3.43
.
∎

Lemma 4.40
.

The geometric product preserves weak equivalences, i.e. if
u
and
v
are weak equivalences, then so is
u\otimes v
.
∎

Corollary 4.41
.

If
u
is a weak equivalence and cofibration and
v
is any map, then the Leibniz geometric product
u\mathbin{\widehat{\otimes}}v
is a weak equivalence.
In particular, cofibrations that are weak equivalences are closed under Leibniz geometric product with cofibrations.
∎

Lemma 4.42
.

There is map of cylinders

\textstyle{I\otimes(A\star B)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{A\star B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{0}\otimes(A\star B)}
\scriptstyle{(\delta_{0}\otimes A)\star(\delta_{0}\otimes B)}
\textstyle{A\star B\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\delta_{1}\otimes(A\star B)}
\scriptstyle{(\delta_{1}\otimes A)\star(\delta_{1}\otimes B)}
\textstyle{(I\otimes A)\star(I\otimes B)}

natural in marked semisimplicial sets
A
and
B
.
∎

Corollary 4.43
.

The join monoidal structure preserves homotopies and homotopy equivalences, i.e. given homotopies
f_{0}\sim f_{1}
and
g_{0}\sim g_{1}
, we have
f_{0}\star g_{0}\sim f_{1}\star g_{1}
, and given homotopy equivalences
u
and
v
, then
u\star v
is a homotopy equivalence.
∎

Corollary 4.44
.

The join monoidal structure preserves weak equivalences, i.e. given weak equivalences
u
and
v
, then
u\star v
is a weak equivalence.
∎

Corollary 4.45
.

The Leibniz join of a cofibration that is a weak equivalence with any map is a weak equivalence.
Cofibrations that are weak equivalences are closed under Leibniz join with cofibrations.
∎

4.7.
Comparison with unmarked semisimplicial sets

Let us relate the homotopical aspects of semisimplicial and marked semisimplicial sets.

The minimal marking functor
(-)^{\flat}
preserves cofibrations, but not weak equivalences (for example, the one-dimensional horn inclusions are not weak equivalences in marked semisimplicial sets).
The marking forgetting functor
(-)^{\mathsf{u}}
preserves cofibrations and anodyne maps.
By adjointness, the maximal marking functor
(-)^{\sharp}
preserves fibrations.

Lemma 4.46
.

The marking forgetting functor preserves weak equivalences.

Proof.

Given a map
f\colon A\to B
of marked semisimplicial sets and a semisimplicial set
X
, note by adjointness that
\hom(f^{\mathsf{u}},X)
is a homotopy equivalence exactly if
\hom(f,X^{\sharp})
is a homotopy equivalence.
Then claim then follows Using the characterizations
lemmas

3.35
and
4.31
of weak equivalences.
∎

The maximal marking functor preserves cofibrations and anodyne maps.

Lemma 4.47
.

The maximal marking functor preserves weak equivalences.

Proof.

This follows directly from our definition of weak equivalencess.
All the ingredients are preserved: anodyne maps, fibrant objects, homotopy equivalences.
∎

4.8.
Comparison with marked simplicial sets

Let us write
(\Delta_{-}^{\prime},\Delta_{+}^{\prime})
for the Reedy structure on
\Delta^{\prime}
.
We write
L\dashv U\dashv R
for the forgetful functor from marked simplicial to marked semisimplicial sets and left and right adjoint.
We denote the induced monad on marked semisimplicial sets by
(UL,\eta,\mu)
.
Recall that
L
sends the geometric to the cartesian symmetric monoidal structure.

The
interval
in marked simplicial sets is given by the image of the semisimplicial inverval under
L
and will be denoted using the same symbols.
Note that it has a contraction and connections.
It is equivalently the image of the interval in simplicial sets under maximal marking.
With the cartesian monoidal structure, this induces associated notions of cylinder, homotopy, homotopy equivalence.
It follows that
L
preserves homotopies and homotopy equivalences.
As in the unmarked case, we also have the corresponding statement for
U
.

Lemma 4.48
.

Functorially in a simplicial set
A
, there is cylinder morphism from the semisimplicial cylinder on
UA
to the image under
U
of the simplicial cylinder on
A
.
∎

Corollary 4.49
.

The forgetful functor
U
functorially preserves homotopies and homotopy equivalences.
∎

The following is analogous to the unmarked case.

Let
S^{\prime}
be the subcategory of
[[1],\Delta^{\prime}]
with objects restricted to maps in
\Delta_{-}^{\prime}
and codomain part of morphisms restricted to maps in
\Delta_{+}^{\prime}
.
We put a coverage it generated by the singleton cover of the identity on
[1]_{\mathsf{m}}
by the identity on
[1]
.
We have a two-sided opfibration

Explicitly, given a marked semisimplicial set

.

The natural transformations

.

of (summits of spans specifiying) linear polynomial functors over

(with indexing of

).

We will construct

as an extension of

An object of the domain of

not in the image of the left map is an edge of the form

\textstyle{[1]_{\mathsf{m}}}

\textstyle{[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

\textstyle{[k]\hbox to0.0pt{.\hss}}

We take the image of the top row object under

and construct the image of the bottom row object under

using a pushout:

\textstyle{[1]\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}

This is easily seen to extend

to

as in (

) and to cohere with the endpoint inclusions as in (

).
Here, recall again that

is a map between discrete Grothendieck fibrations over

, hence it is enough to define

on objects and check a coherence condition on morphisms.c
∎

4.9.
Comparison with quasicategories

Consider weak factorization systems (cofibration, trivial fibration) and (anodyne map, naive fibration) in marked simplicial sets induced by the corresponding ones in marked semisimplicial sets via the adjunction
L\dashv U
.
Note that a map is a cofibration exactly if it is a cofibration of underlying simplicial sets (i.e. a monomorphism with decidable latching object inclusions) and the inclusion of markings is decidable.
Using classical logic, there is a Cisinski model structure on marked simplicial sets where the fibrant objects and fibrations between fibrant objects coincide with the naive ones, so will be drop the adjective ‘naive’ in these cases.
Except for an unimportant detail (marking only edges rather than simplices in all dimensions), this is the model structure for weak 1-complicial sets of Verity
[
Ver08
]
.
Using the established classical theory, it is easily seen that
(-)^{\flat}\dashv(-)^{\mathsf{u}}
forms a Quillen equivalence from the Joyal model structure for quasicategories to the model structure for weak 1-complicial sets.
Recall that the action of the left derived functor of
(-)^{\flat}
on fibrant objects is the
natural marking

[
Lur09
]
functor
(-)^{\natural}
that marks the equivalences, providing explicit (1-categorical) equivalences in both directions between fibration categories of fibrant objects.
Instead of directly comparing fibrant marked semisimplicial sets with quasicategories, it will thus suffice to relate the homotopy theories of marked semisimplicial and marked simplicial sets.

The following development is analogous to the unmarked case.
The omitted proofs are adapted verbatim.

Lemma 4.51
.

The map
\operatorname{Sk}^{n}(1)\to\operatorname{Sk}^{n+2}(1)
is a weak equivalence for even
n
.

Proof.

By
lemma

4.47
.
∎

Corollary 4.52
.

The map
\top\to 1
is a cofibration and a weak equivalence.

Proof.

By
lemma

4.47
.
∎

Lemma 4.53
.

The unit
\eta\colon\operatorname{Id}\to UL
is valued in cofibrations that are weak equivalences.
∎

Proof.

As in the proof of
lemma

3.65
, it will suffice to show that the components of
\eta
on
\Delta^{n}
for
n\geq 0
and
(\Delta^{1})^{\sharp}
are weak equivalences.
The cases of
\Delta^{0}
and
(\Delta^{1})^{\sharp}
follow from
lemma

3.65
by maximal marking.
The case of
\Delta^{n}
for
n\geq 1
reduces to the one for
n=0
by
corollary

3.50
and monoidality of
L
and
U
with respect to the join.
∎

Corollary 4.54
.

The functor
UL
creates weak equivalences.
∎

Corollary 4.55
.

U
sends anodyne maps to cofibrations that are weak equivalences.
∎

Lemma 4.56
.

The functor
U
sends trivial fibrations to weak equivalences.
∎

The following statements presuppose the model structure on marked simplicial sets, which requires classical logic.

Corollary 4.57

(Classical logic)
.

The functor
U
preserves weak equivalences.

Proof.

Using fibrant replacement in the model structure on marked simplicial sets, every weak equivalences admits a morphism in the arrow category consisting of anodyne maps to a trivial fibration.
The claim then follows by
corollaries

4.55
,
4.56
and
4.20
.
∎

Corollary 4.58

(Classical logic)
.

The functor
L
creates weak equivalences.
∎

We conclude as in the unmarked case.

Theorem 4.59

(Classical logic)
.

In the triple of functors

\textstyle{(\widehat{\Delta_{+}})_{\mathsf{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\scriptstyle{\bot}
\textstyle{\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathsf{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{U}

relating marked semisimplicial sets and marked simplicial sets where
U
is the forgetful functor:

•

the maps
L
and
U
are weak equivalences of cofibration categories,

•

the maps
U
and
R
, restricted to fibrant objects, are weak equivalences of fibration categories,

•

both adjunctions descend to equivalences of homotopy categories.

Proof.

Analogous to
theorem

3.73
.
∎

Composing with the Quillen equivalence between the model structures on marked simplicial sets and simplicial sets for higher categories, we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 4.60

(Classical logic)
.

In the composite of adjunctions

\textstyle{(\widehat{\Delta_{+}})_{{\mathsf{m}},{\mathsf{fib}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{R}
\scriptstyle{\top}
\textstyle{\widehat{\Delta}_{{\mathsf{m}},{\mathsf{fib}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{U}
\scriptstyle{(-)^{\mathsf{u}}}
\scriptstyle{\top}
\textstyle{\widehat{\Delta}_{{\mathsf{fib}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(-)^{\natural}}

relating marked semisimplicial sets and simplicial sets where
U
and
(-)^{\mathsf{u}}
are the forgetful functors:

•

the left adjoints are weak equivalences of cofibration categories,

•

the right adjoints, restricted to fibrant objects, are weak equivalences of fibration categories,

•

the adjunctions descend to equivalences of homotopy categories,

•

restricted to fibrant objects, the functor
U
is and the functor
(-)^{\mathsf{u}}
has a weakly equivalent replacement
(-)^{\natural}
that is a weak equivalence of fibration categories.
∎

As in the unmarked case, we have a map
A\times B\to A\otimes B
natural in marked semisimplicial sets
A
and
B
.

Lemma 4.61
.

For fibrant
X
, the map
\eta_{X}\colon X\to ULX
is a homotopy equivalence.
∎

Theorem 4.62
.

Given fibrant marked semisimplicial sets
X
and
Y
, the canonical comparison map

X\times Y\to X\otimes Y

is a weak equivalence.

Proof.

Analogous to the proof of
theorem

3.75
.
∎

4.10.
Subdivision

Everything in this section is an evident extension of the unmarked case.
Let us write
L\dashv U
for both the free (marked) category on a (marked) semicategory and the free (marked) simplicial set on a (marked) semisimplicial set adjunction.

4.10.1.
Marked simplicial subdivision

The category of elements construction on simplicial sets extends to a marked category of elements construction

{\textstyle{\int}\vphantom{\int}_{\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathsf{m}}}}\colon\widehat{\Delta}_{\mathsf{m}}\to\mathbf{Cat}_{\mathsf{m}}

by marking those morphisms that
{\textstyle{\int}\vphantom{\int}_{\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathsf{m}}}}A
that correspond to a marked edge in
A
under evaluation at the last vertex.

The simplicial subdivision functor extends to marked simplicial sets by letting the markings of
\operatorname{Sd}A
be created via the last vertex map
\operatorname{Sd}A\to A
.
By definition, one thus also has a last vertex map in the marked setting.
Using the same argument as in the unmarked case, this map can classically shown to be a weak equivalence in the model structure on marked simplicial sets we have considered in
section

3.7
.

4.10.2.
Marked semisimplicial subdivision

Recall the semicategory of elements construction

{\textstyle{\int}\vphantom{\int}_{\hskip-1.63885pt+}}\colon\widehat{\Delta_{+}}\to\mathbf{SemiCat}.

It extends to a
marked semicategory of elements
construction

{\textstyle{\int}\vphantom{\int}_{\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathsf{m}},+}}\colon(\widehat{\Delta_{+}})_{\mathsf{m}}\to\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}}

by marking those morphisms
f\colon([m],x)\to([n],y)
in
{\textstyle{\int}\vphantom{\int}_{\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathsf{m}},+}}(A,W)
that preserve initial objects under the forgetful map to
\Delta_{+}
, i.e.
f(0)=0
, or otherwise have
A(g)(y)\in W
where
g\colon[1]\to[n]
sends 0 to
0
and 1 to
f(0)
.

Say for a moment that a marked simplicial or semisimplicial set
(A,W)
is closed under 2-out-of-3 if it lifts against the Leibniz applications of the unit of the maximal marking monad to
h^{2}_{i}
for
0\leq i\leq 2
; say it is closed under 2-out-of-6 if it is closed under 2-out-of-3 and lifts against the marking saturation inclusion.
Then
{\textstyle{\int}\vphantom{\int}_{\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathsf{m}},+}}
preserves taking the closure of markings under 2-out-of-3 and 2-out-of-6, respectively.
In particular, the marked semicategory
{\textstyle{\int}\vphantom{\int}_{\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathsf{m}},+}}(A,W)
is closed under 2-out-of-3 and 2-out-of-6, respectively, exactly if
(A,W)
is.

Analogous to the unmarked case, the marked semisimplicial subdivision functor
\operatorname{Sd}
is defined as the composite of the marked semicategory of elements functor
{\textstyle{\int}\vphantom{\int}_{\hskip-1.63885pt{\mathsf{m}},+}}
followed by the marked semisimplicial nerve.
On underlying semisimplicial sets, it is given by the semisimplicial subdivision functor, so the only new thing is the action on markings.
Recall that colimits in marked semisimplicial sets are computed as colimits in underlying semisimplicial sets with an edge marked if it comes from a marked edge via a colimit coprojection.
We see that marked semisimplicial subdivision is cocontinuous and its action
\operatorname{sd}
on simplices (including the marked edge) is described by the composite

\textstyle{\Delta_{+}^{\prime}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\Delta_{+})_{/-}}
\textstyle{\mathbf{SemiCat}_{\mathsf{m}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{N}
\textstyle{(\widehat{\Delta_{+}})_{\mathsf{m}}}

where the first arrow sends
[n]\in\Delta_{+}^{\prime}
to the categorical slice
(\Delta_{+})_{/[n]}
with markings created by the last vertex functor
(\Delta_{+})_{/[n]}\to[n]
of categories.
As in the unmarked case, from this one sees immediately that the free marked simplicial set functor commutes with subdivision.

4.10.3.
Relating
A
and
\operatorname{Sd}A

Everything we have said in the unmarked setting about relating a semisimplicial set with its subdivision via a cospan of weak equivalences lifts to the marked setting.
Given a marked semisimplicial set
A
, we have a cospan

\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{A}}
\textstyle{\operatorname{Sd}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{ULA}

of weak equivalences.
Here, the left leg is a cofibration and weak equivalence by
lemma

4.53
.
The right leg is the composite

\textstyle{\operatorname{Sd}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\eta_{\operatorname{Sd}A}}
\scriptstyle{\sim}
\textstyle{UL\operatorname{Sd}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{m}
\textstyle{ULA}

where
m
is
U
applied to the marked simplicial last vertex map on
LA
.
Again we argue that
m
is a homotopy equivalence when
A
comes from
\Delta_{+}^{\prime}
and use cocontinuity and the glueing lemma in the standard way to show that
m
is a weak equivalence for all
A
.

We will now lift the finiteness preserving cospan construction (
3.22
) to the marked setting.
For this, we first define a marked version of the indexed join that also combines the join and the marking join in one operation.
The input consists of marked semisimplicial sets
A
and
B
with functors

F,F_{\mathsf{m}}\colon{\textstyle\int}A\times{\textstyle\int}B

with a monomorphism
F_{\mathsf{m}}\to F
that we write as an inclusion.
The output
A*_{F,F_{\mathsf{m}}}B
has as underlying semisimplicial set the indexed join
A^{\mathsf{u}}*_{F}B^{\mathsf{u}}
with those edges marked that come from a marked edge in
A
or
B
or an element
t\in F_{\mathsf{m}}(([0],x),([0],y))
with
x\in A_{0}
and
y\in B_{0}
.

Given a marked semisimplicial set
A
, note that we have subfunctor
\hom_{\mathsf{m}}
of the hom-functor of
{\textstyle\int}A
that returns only the marked morphisms in
{\textstyle\int}_{\mathsf{m}}A
.
This induces a subfunctor
F_{m}(A)
of
F(A^{\mathsf{u}})
.
We then obtain a Reedy cofibrant span

(4.16)

\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.5pt\hbox{\kern 34.69142pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&&\cr&\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-6.75pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-8.58891pt\raise-17.08153pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota_{0}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 6.5331pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern 5.54688pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern 10.34375pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\operatorname{Sd}A\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 15.5981pt\raise-17.96165pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.00694pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{\iota_{1}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 11.88377pt\raise-23.99998pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-3.0pt\raise-31.44443pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{}$}}}}}}}{\hbox{\kern-34.69142pt\raise-31.44443pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.5pt\hbox{$\textstyle{A\star_{F(A^{\mathsf{u}}),F_{\mathsf{m}}(A)}\operatorname{Sd}A}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

functorial and cocontinuous in the marked semisimplicial set
A
.

We now prove analogues of
lemmas

3.76
and
3.77
.
We will only discuss the changes to the unmarked setting.

Lemma 4.63
.

Pushout application of the left leg of (
4.16
) sends cofibrations to anodyne maps.

Proof.

By cocontinuity, one once again reduces to boundary inclusions and the edge marking inclusion.
The second case is easy, using that the marking satural inclusion is anodyne.

In the first case, we have to make sure that the marked analogue of the map (
3.24
) (with
\star^{\prime}
defined in terms of
\star_{F(A^{\mathsf{u}}),F_{\mathsf{m}}(A)}
instead of
\star_{F}
) is anodyne.
In the presentation of (
3.24
) as a pushout of (
3.25
), the edge from
\tau_{0}(k)
to
\tau_{1}(0)
of the simplex
\Delta^{k}\star\Delta^{m}
is mapped to a marked edge in
\Delta^{n}\star^{\prime}\Delta^{m}
as the last vertex of
f(0)
is exactly
k
.
But (
3.25
) with that edge marked is the Leibniz join of the boundary inclusion of dimension
k-1
(omitted if
k=0
) with the marked left outer inclusion of dimension
m+1
, hence anodyne by
lemma

4.7
.
∎

Lemma 4.64
.

Pushout application of the right leg of (
4.16
) sends cofibrations to anodyne maps.

Proof.

We once again only discuss the case of a boundary inclusion.

The bottom square of (
3.27
), with
\star^{\prime}
redefined as in the proof of (
4.63
), is a pushout of (
3.28
) with edge from
\tau_{0}(n)
to
\tau_{1}(0
) marked (the attaching map
\Delta^{0}\to\operatorname{Sd}\Delta^{n}
corresponds to the object
([n],\operatorname{id})
of
(\Delta_{+})_{/[n]}
and has last vertex
n
) and hence anodyne.

For the top square of (
3.27
), it will suffice to verify that the pushout corner map in (
3.29
) remains anodyne in marked semisimplicial sets; we claim that it lies in the saturation of inner horn inclusions.
This was the Leibniz join of
\partial\Delta^{k}\to\Delta^{k}
with the nerve of (
3.29
).
Let us call a semisimplicial map left anodyne if it lies in the weak saturation of inner and left outer horn inclusions.
The Leibniz geometric product of a left anodyne map with a cofibration is again left anodyne, by the analysis of
lemma

3.7
given in the proof of (
4.6
).
In particular, the iterated Leibniz geometric product of
\delta_{0}
that is the nerve of (
3.29
) is left anodyne.
The claim then follows since Leibniz join with
\partial\Delta^{k}\to\Delta^{k}
from the left sends left outer horn inclusions to inner horn inclusions and preserves inner horn inclusions.
∎

Corollary 4.65
.

The legs of (
4.16
) are anodyne for all semisimplicial sets
A
.

Proof.

By
lemmas

4.63
and
4.64
.
∎
